### 0.22 D-042 closed — field-sensitive moves and exact destruction

**Classification:** compiler defect. `[EXP-6]` already permits moving a field
out of a plain owned struct and says that the aggregate becomes partially
moved. `[OWN-2]`, `[OWN-3]`, and `[DRP-2]` already require each value to be
destroyed exactly once and remaining fields in the prescribed order. Neither
the adopted 0.8.5 source nor frozen H8 was changed.

**What was wrong.** `moved_by_operand` retained only a local ID and explicitly
discarded every projected move. For:

```ember
pair = Pair(Part(1), Part(2))
first = pair.first
```

`first` destroyed `Part(1)`, then the whole-local scope drop visited
`pair.second` and the moved-from `pair.first`; output was `1, 2, 1` for two
values. The old state could express only `Live`, `Moved`, or `Maybe` for the
entire local, so it could neither preserve a sibling nor attach a flag to one
conditional field.

**Compiler mechanism.** `compiler/ember_analysis/src/drops.rs` now builds a
recursive move-path tree for each MIR local. Its powerset state retains live,
wholly moved, and partially moved possibilities across joins. A projected move
invalidates only its subtree; writes reinitialise that subtree and recompute
its ancestors. A partial aggregate drop expands into independently droppable
live paths in `[DRP-2]` order, while conditional paths receive distinct flags.
Moves in call terminators clear those flags too. A sibling path remains usable;
a whole-value use while an ancestor is partial emits `E3042`/O4, documented in
`docs/errors/E3042.md`.

**Adversarial evidence.** Six new `tests/conformance/EXP-6/` programs cover:

1. exact unconditional destructor count (`1, 2`, never `1, 2, 1`);
2. a conditional field flag on moved and unmoved paths;
3. a projected move passed through a call terminator;
4. nested move paths and disjoint sibling reads;
5. field reinitialisation restoring whole-value usability; and
6. rejection of whole-value use as `E3042` while the sibling remains legal.

The pre-fix compiler failed the first and conditional probes. Deliberately
removing projected-move collection after the fix makes the conformance suite
red again. Generated C for the minimal case contains only
`em_Part_drop(&_2)` and `em_Part_drop(&_1.second)`—no drop of
`_1.first`.

**Adjacent defect found by the probe: D-043, also fixed.** The call-terminator
case initially produced no destructor for the transferred value at all. This
was independent of field flags: an `owned` parameter had never been registered
in the callee's ownership scope. MIR lowering now treats owned, droppable
parameters as the function's outer ownership scope; explicit return and
fallthrough both discharge it unless the argument moved onward. Parameter
drop flags start live. `tests/conformance/OWN-2/accept_owned_parameters_drop_in_the_callee.em`
pins both paths. This too was a clear compiler violation of `[FN-1]` and
`[OWN-2]`, so no specification or ADR moved.

**Scope report.** Approved work was D-042. Actual work was D-042 plus D-043,
because the required D-042 call-transfer probe exposed the independent callee
leak. D-043 was recorded separately rather than folded into D-042. No next
feature block was started.

### 0.23 Cell/RefCell conformance closure and D-044 — 2026-09-12

**Classification.** `[CELL-6a]` and `[CELL-9]` lacked durable coverage. The
`[CELL-10]` probe then exposed a separate compiler defect, D-044. The adopted
specification already made an omitted parameter mode a shared borrow (`[FN-1]`),
mapped E3023 to shape B4, and constrained RefCell suggestions. The compiler and
tests moved; neither `ember-spec.md`, frozen 0.8.5, nor frozen H8 changed, and no
ADR was needed.

**The defect and minimal reproducer.** A default-mode value parameter was
represented as an ordinary ABI local, so this compiled:

```ember
struct Counter:
    pub value: i32

fn increment(counter: Counter):
    counter.value = counter.value + 1
```

The assignment changed only the callee's private copy and silently disappeared
on return. This was not merely an assignment-parser hole: explicit `ref mut`,
`as_mut_span()`, a `mut self` method, and forwarding to a `mut` parameter all
formed write-capable access through the same borrowed mode.

**Compiler mechanism.** Type checking now retains a set of default-mode
parameter locals independently of their physical ABI types, including generic
functions, methods, and lambda bodies. Every mutable-access creation path walks
its HIR place to the root local and emits classified E3023/B4 when that root is
a borrowed parameter. Shared-reference writes retain E3021 and are not double-
reported. The first E3023 help is always the structural repair — one owner and
an explicit `mut` parameter. At a single access proven non-simultaneous, later
helps may name `Cell[T]` for a `Copy` payload, `RefCell[T]`, then class, with
their costs. Method and argument boundaries conservatively omit RefCell until
the whole call can prove that sibling accesses are not simultaneous. This is
`[CELL-10]`'s distinction, not a universal interior-mutability recommendation.

**Harness mechanism.** Three new annotations make the relevant contracts
executable rather than prose:

* `#$ profiles: debug, release, shipping` repeats check/build/run and uses a
  separate output directory per profile; tests without it retain the prior
  debug-only behavior;
* repeated `#$ help:` directives must occur in that order in rendered help
  lines; and
* `#$ not-help:` forbids a substring specifically in rendered help lines, so a
  source annotation cannot satisfy or defeat itself.

**Conformance evidence.** `tests/conformance/CELL-6a/` exercises both fallible
borrow directions and observes `None` in all three profiles.
`tests/conformance/CELL-9/` inspects generated C for `ptrdiff_t borrow;` — the
counter is one machine word and `[CELL-5]`'s location fields are separate — and
provokes the runtime check in all three profiles. The
`exclusivity = "unchecked"` sentence concerns dynamic classes; package/class
exclusivity is not a reachable compiler feature yet, so that interaction is a
dependency gap rather than a defect or a falsely claimed test.
`tests/conformance/CELL-10/` contains one structural accept, five independent
E3023 access-path rejects, and a simultaneous-borrow E3022 case that forbids a
RefCell help. `docs/errors/E3023.md` provides an executable failing example and
compilable repair.

**Mutation evidence.** The minimal assignment test was red before the compiler
fix. Disabling parameter-provenance enforcement made all five E3023 probes
compile with exit 0. Reversing the required help order made the suite fail on
the order assertion. Running the every-profile case as shipping-only with an
incorrect output failed and named `[shipping]`. Replacing the C counter needle
with `size_t borrow;` failed and printed the actual `ptrdiff_t` field. These
checks were restored before the green run.

**Scope report.** Approved work was the bounded `[CELL-6a]`/`[CELL-9]`/
`[CELL-10]` closure. Actual work included the harness support that those rules
require and D-044 because the mandatory `[CELL-10]` probe demonstrated a live
compiler violation of `[FN-1]`; it was filed and fixed rather than hidden in a
coverage task. Stale type-checker comments about decided ERR-041/UnsafeCell and
the ERR-029 summary/body mismatch were corrected as documentation only. D-038,
Arena, UnsafeCell, H8 adoption, and multi-region views were not started.

**Superseded pickup.** D-038 is now closed in §0.24 and Arena core is complete
in §0.25. Current work begins at §0.14 and `docs/MIGRATION-0.9.6.md`; this
paragraph is retained only as the earlier checkpoint.

### 0.24 D-038 closed — implicit `String`→`str` borrows — 2026-09-12

**Classification:** compiler defect. `[SPN-1]` already states that `String`
coerces to `str` and that the coercion takes a borrow. The checker instead
reported E2020. The rule was unambiguous, so neither the adopted specification,
frozen H8 target, nor an ADR changed.

**Mechanism:** `coerce` recognizes the compiler-known `String` representation
when `str` is expected and calls the same `view_of(..., StringAsStr)` producer
as explicit `as_str()`. This preserves the explicit HIR/MIR reference, region
elision, `verify_views` backstop, and backend path established by D-037; there
is no second view-producing implementation.

**Evidence:** three cases in `tests/conformance/SPN-1/` cover direct binding and
argument coercion, NLL ending a call-only borrow before later mutation, E3021
when mutation overlaps a live implicit view, and E3060 when code attempts to
return a view of non-view parameter storage. Before the fix the acceptance case
and mutation case failed at the coercion with E2020. Removing only the new arm
afterward restored E2020 in all three probes; restoring it made the full 200-
case conformance suite green.

**Scope report:** approved work was D-038. Actual work was the coercion arm,
three adversarial tests, and the defect/current-state documentation. No Arena,
UnsafeCell, normative-specification, frozen-H8, or multi-region implementation
was included in this bounded change. The owner subsequently gave a standing
instruction to continue, so Arena is the current pickup without a separate
pause.

### 0.25 Arena core and H9 return provenance — 2026-09-12

**Scope and authority.** The standing instruction was to continue the 0.9.5
implementation through Arena and stop at a real issue. Implementation exposed
one genuine specification boundary: H8 `[LT-4]` required a wrapper's returned
Arena allocation to retain the Arena region, but `[LT-1a]` prohibited naming
the non-view Arena parameter in `@borrows`. Work stopped; the owner supplied
the narrow Arena-backed return-provenance ruling; it was frozen as
`docs/spec-source/Ember_v0.9.5_Hardened_9.md` (718,316 bytes, 7,696 lines,
SHA-256 `0DAC71E2832FE422FFEBE004E25B438CE41BB518E921C6D8B243C8FE5F6A1738`).
ODR-008/ERR-049 are closed and
ADR-028 records the decision. H8 remains byte-for-byte frozen. The adopted
normative source remains `docs/spec-source/ember-spec.md`
(`0.8.5_Hardened_1`) until the explicit 0.9.5 adoption gate.

**Implemented compiler/runtime core:**

| Surface | Evidence-backed state |
|---|---|
| `Arena.with_capacity(bytes)` | compiler-known, move-only owner of opaque chunk state; compiler drop frees chunks |
| `Arena.alloc(owned value)` | stable, aligned bump allocation; shared Arena borrow is carried by the returned `ref mut T`; types needing drop are E3090 |
| `Arena.alloc_nodrop(owned value)` | same allocation path, explicitly permits byte-reclamation without running the value's destructor |
| `Arena.reset()` | mutably borrows the Arena and rewinds retained chunks; live allocation views conflict through ordinary borrow analysis |
| `Arena.fixed(MutSpan[u8])` / `FixedArena` | uses caller storage, never grows or owns the backing buffer, respects absolute alignment, and panics on exhaustion (including an empty buffer) |
| `Arena.scope()` / `ScopedArena` | nested arbitrary-depth LIFO marks; the guard holds the immediate parent's mutable borrow; drop rewinds; conflicting parent use is E3096 with the `[ARN-6]` help |
| H9 wrappers | `@borrows(arena)` is accepted only for a growing Arena parameter and a returned view actually backed by it; omitted provenance is E3061, false provenance is E3062, and arbitrary non-view parameters remain E2031 |
| Generic `[ARN-3]` checking | concrete generic instantiations replay monomorphisation-dependent E3090, so `alloc(Array[T]())` cannot pass merely because opaque `T` appeared drop-free during the first check |

The runtime uses an opaque linked chain of non-moving chunks. Growth appends a
chunk, reset retains and rewinds chunks, and scope marks identify an exact bump
position. The backend lowers these operations deterministically to
`ember_arena_*` / `ember_fixed_arena_*`; no source-spellable private-field API
or invalid C identifier was introduced. Generated C is exercised by the
conformance harness under C11 warnings, and the final manual gate includes
`-pedantic` as required by §0.0 K.

**Why the wrapper mechanism matters.** A default-mode Arena parameter is not a
view and remains ABI-by-value today. A direct call returning an Arena-backed
view therefore has no ordinary `Rvalue::Ref` for the caller to observe. The
borrow analysis now creates a narrow signature-derived shared loan only when
the callee's elision contract ties that return to a growing Arena argument.
That keeps caller-local escape, reset, and drop checks load-bearing without
making Arena a view or generalising `@borrows` to arbitrary values.

**Conformance:** 29 new cases bring the suite to 229 cases in 80 rule
directories. Dedicated directories cover `[ARN-1]`, `[ARN-2]`, `[ARN-3]`,
`[ARN-4]`, `[ARN-6]`, `[ARN-7]`, `[LT-4]`, `[LT-4a]`, `[LT-4b]`, and
`[TST-22]`. The cases include multiple outstanding allocations, move/use after
move, reset conflicts and reuse, fixed storage/alignment/exhaustion, nested
scope conflicts and LIFO rewind, direct/nested wrappers, omitted and false
provenance, caller escape, and generic drop/no-drop substitutions. This is
core evidence, not a claim that every clause of `[ARN-*]` is implemented.

**Important adversarial lesson.** The first generic implementation checked
`arena.alloc(value: T)` while `T` was opaque, concluded that `T` did not need
drop, and discarded E3090 from the concrete instantiation's intentionally
quiet diagnostic sink. A three-line generic call with `Array[i32]` exposed the
hole before commit. The repair is a concrete-instantiation semantic check, not
a blanket rejection of generic Arena allocation. This repeats the standing
rule: passing ordinary examples is necessary but not evidence that a generic
or ownership invariant is complete.

**Intentionally incomplete dependency gaps — not compiler defects:**

* `[ARN-3]` `alloc_array` requires the actual `Default` and `Zeroable`
  interfaces, neither of which exists yet.
* `[ARN-3]` `alloc_uninit` requires a defined `MaybeUninit` type and unsafe
  initialization boundary; none may be invented locally for Arena.
* `[ARN-5]` `ArenaArray`/`ArenaMap` require their collection/generic
  infrastructure and remain absent; the H9 rule-index report names ARN-5 as a
  missing conformance directory.
* effect/`@noalloc`, `@must_drop`, `Send`/`Sync`, and `[JOB-5]` `ThreadArena`
  obligations wait for their specified later phases.
* `[TST-22]`'s mutable-span wrapper clause remains dependent on the unfinished
  bulk-allocation surface; the existing cases prove `ref mut` provenance but
  must not be described as complete TST-22 conformance.

These gaps are tracked as `ARN-INIT-1`, `ARN-COLL-1`, and `ARN-LATE-1` in
`BACKLOG.md`. Do not weaken `[ARN-*]`, fake a `Default`/`Zeroable` bound, or
represent `MaybeUninit` as ordinary initialized bytes merely to finish the API.

**Next-boundary result at H9 freeze:** the normative audit did find unsafe-
initialization and API ambiguity, so work stopped exactly as required. Section
0.26 preserves that question and §0.27 records its H10 resolution. It is no
longer an owner blocker. `UnsafeCell` follows the completed Arena milestone;
the 0.9.5 multi-region-view work follows explicit target adoption.

### 0.26 ODR-009 — historical Arena initialization blocker, resolved in H10

After `d2ec959`, the next dependency audit read H9 `[ARN-3]`, `[UNS-1]`, and
`[UNS-5]` together. The document names `Default`, `Zeroable`, `MaybeUninit`,
`alloc_array`, and `alloc_uninit`, but does not define the safety contract an
implementation needs:

* `MaybeUninit[T]` has no normative layout, drop/copy, write, or
  assume-initialized/span-transition surface;
* “all-scalar/POD” does not safely define `Zeroable` — a scalar-shaped
  non-null reference has an invalid all-zero representation, and range/enum
  validity can exclude zero too;
* `alloc_array` does not state its exact bound/fallback, neither-capability
  diagnostic, partial-`Default` failure behavior, or how `[ARN-3]`'s
  `needs_drop` ban applies;
* Arena is Phase 2, while the plan schedules `Zeroable` derives in Phase 4.

The minimal current compiler probe
`pixels = arena.alloc_array[Pixel](2)` reports E1010 “only direct calls are
supported in this phase”. That exposes the separate compiler limitation that
explicit generic arguments on methods are absent; it is now `GEN-METHOD-1`.
Fixing that mechanism would only reach the unsafe-initialization ambiguity, not
answer it.

**Classification at discovery:** ERR-050 was a specification/API safety gap;
ODR-009 was a P1 owner decision. H9 is frozen and was not edited. This was not a compiler defect,
and no provisional `MaybeUninit`, byte-zero whitelist, or diagnostic ID was
invented. `BACKLOG.md` tracks the implementation dependencies as
`ARN-INIT-1`, `ARN-COLL-1`, `ARN-LATE-1`, and `GEN-METHOD-1`.

**Resolution:** the owner supplied the complete allocation/initialization
contract and then its canonical public API on 2026-09-12. H10 incorporates the
ruling; ADR-029, HC-095-09, ERR-050, and the closed ODR-009 preserve it. No
compiler implementation was begun as part of the H10 documentation cut.

### 0.27 H10 Arena allocation / initialization contract — 2026-09-12

`docs/spec-source/Ember_v0.9.5_Hardened_10.md` is the new frozen development
target (728,498 bytes, 7,765 lines, SHA-256
`F3FFF9C14AA3C80E57949479A1BC917FE7DF62B551B7CB8B17669D19FC5C4B14`).
H9 remains unchanged. The adopted normative source remains
`docs/spec-source/ember-spec.md` (`0.8.5_Hardened_1`) until explicit adoption.

H10 fixes the previously missing boundary:

* ordinary `alloc_array[T]` always requires `!needs_drop(T)`, chooses
  `Zeroable` before `Default`, and reports existing E2040 when neither applies;
* Default construction is transactional with respect to the Arena cursor;
* `Zeroable` means exactly all-zero representation validity and carries no
  unrelated ownership/thread/FFI implications;
* `MaybeUninit[T]` has `T`'s size/alignment, never drops `T`, and is `Copy` iff
  `T` is;
* canonical safe `uninit`/`write`/`write_at` and unsafe consuming
  `assume_init` transitions define the value and full-span boundary;
* core `Zeroable` support belongs to Phase 2; general derive generation may
  remain Phase 4.

The owner feed reused `[ARN-4]`–`[ARN-7]`, which H9 had already frozen for
bump allocation, Arena collections, scopes, and LIFO safety. H10 preserves
those meanings and uses `[ARN-8]`–`[ARN-13]`; `[TST-ARN-MU]` becomes numeric
`[TST-23]`. The supplied API prose said “moves” and “consumes” while its sketch
omitted modes; H10 spells `owned value`, `mut self`, and `owned self`
explicitly so `[FN-2]`'s borrowed default does not contradict the ruling.

**Evidence boundary.** This pass changes specification and ledgers only. The
alternate H10 rule-index audit finds 960 rules, 854 stated definitions, no
duplicate definitions, and no orphaned amendments. Its inherited four dangling
references remain `IDE-2`, `IDE-5`, `IDE-10`, and `HOT-10`. The new
`ARN-8`–`ARN-13`/`TST-23` conformance directories and compiler/runtime support
do not exist yet, so H10 is not claimed conformant. The six repository gates
continue to validate the adopted 0.8.5 source, not automatic H10 adoption.

drop-before-store after RHS evaluation, `Cell.set` remains store-before-drop,
and `MaybeUninit.write` stores without dropping prior bytes.

`T.default()` has no recoverable failure channel and panic terminates through

or observable rollback is required. Rollback applies only where a separate API
defines both a recoverable construction-failure path and transactional
rollback. H1 amends `[ARN-10]` itself; no `[ARN-10a]` was invented.

### 0.29 H1 implementation checkpoint — 2026-09-13

This checkpoint is **uncommitted** on `main`; HEAD and `origin/main` remain
`adfd5eac42f7af3e85e688b1bf1d001ff20cf71d`. The working tree contains both
the H1 specification/ledger cut and implementation changes. Do not describe it
as clean or pushed until a later commit actually exists.

**Canonical-fact slice.** `TypeIdentity` and `LayoutDescriptor` name the
existing canonical type/layout representations. `compiler/ember_analysis/src/facts.rs`
defines the first load-bearing `BorrowCapability`/`AccessContract` axes and
shared `InitializationState`; Arena loans distinguish provenance root from
allocation storage identity. `OwnershipGraph`, `EffectSet`, complete
producer/consumer migration, callable summaries, invalidation, and erasure
evidence remain open under `ARCH-096-1`. No placeholder fact is counted as an
implementation merely because its type name exists.

**Generic and associated calls.** `GEN-METHOD-1` is complete.
Source-declared generic methods and associated functions now share ordinary
generic-call inference, explicit arguments, bounds, callable expectations,
interface conformance/defaults, monomorphisation, and deterministic instance
symbols. Generic owners preserve both owner and method parameters; return
provenance is rebased after the concrete receiver is restored. Receiver-less
interface members remain represented separately from value methods: concrete
`Type.default()` and bound `T.default()` calls resolve and execute.
Interface conformance checks the full receiver/parameter/return contract after
`Self`, associated-type, and alpha-equivalent generic substitution rather
than accepting a matching name.

**Initialization/Arena.** Compiler-known `MaybeUninit[T]` has `T`'s exact
size/alignment, suppresses field destruction, is `Copy` iff `T` is, and exposes
only `uninit`, no-old-drop `write`, and unsafe consuming `assume_init`.
`Arena.alloc_uninit[T]`, span `write_at`, and unsafe consuming span
`assume_init` use runtime allocation, ordinary bounds checks, Arena-region
retention, and the canonical transition boundary. `alloc_array[T]` checks
`!needs_drop(T)` first, chooses a conservative compiler-proven `Zeroable`
path before `Default`, and reports E2040 when neither capability exists. Its
`Default` path reserves raw storage and lowers an explicit MIR loop that calls
the resolved constructor once per element. `[ARN-10]` has success and
all-profile abort/no-continuation evidence; the compiler introduces no
unwinding and no post-abort Arena observation.

The complete `[TST-23]` matrix now covers scalar/span writes, unsafe consuming
conversion, partial/full initialization boundaries, layout, ownership transfer,
destruction, zero validity, capability precedence, fallback diagnostics, and
abort-only panic behavior. General/manual or derived `Zeroable` support remains
assigned to its later phase. Unsupported validity is never guessed from field
shape outside the currently proved built-in predicate.

**Cell dependency closure.** `CELL-DEF-1` is complete. `Cell.take` constructs
the selected default replacement before moving out the old payload.
Non-`Copy` `Cell.update` evaluates source operands first, moves the old payload
behind a default placeholder before invoking the default-borrowed callback,
then stores the callback result before dropping the placeholder. This keeps
the cell initialized across callback/destructor re-entry. The existing Copy
arm remains separate. `CELL-SYNC-1` remains open because `Send`/`Sync` and
threading do not exist yet.

**Defects found while building the mechanism.** D-045 fixed Cell receiver/
argument evaluation order. D-046 made interface conformance and imported
default bodies whole-program and qualification-correct. D-047 made interface
implementation signatures real contracts rather than name-only checks. Each
has a mutation-tested executable case in `DEFECTS.md`; the frozen H1 target and
adopted specification were already clear and did not change. D-048 through
D-050 then exposed three independent generic-pipeline holes: explicit
arguments lost to literal defaults, solved types not reaching callable
expectations, and substitution/inference stopping at spans. Their permanent
adversarial cases also cover argument-order independence and generic-owner
return provenance. The governing implementation lesson is that a generic fact
is not complete until every dependent consumer receives it.

**Evidence at this checkpoint:** `cargo test --workspace` passes all **183**
Rust tests; the full conformance runner is green over **86** top-level rule
directories and **274** `.em` files including support modules. The test build
retains one pre-existing non-snake-case test-name warning; this is distinct
from the warning-free ordinary build. The ordinary build, all six adopted-source
gates, and `git diff --check` are green. The configured MSVC toolchain compiled
the generated C; a separate `-pedantic` pass remains unavailable because this
host exposes no `clang`, `gcc`, or `cl` executable on its normal `PATH`.

**Exact next task:** obtain ODR-011's three residual owner details for

Continue `ARCH-096-1` through real producers and consumers. Do not

### 0.33 H4 Hash and ArenaMap implementation checkpoint — 2026-09-13

This section supersedes §0.32's implementation-status and exact-next-task
statements. H4 itself remains frozen and unchanged. The implementation landed
as `825eac53385bf58c2e311ecce58629f2d5a8ef0f` (`Complete H4 ArenaMap hashing
boundary`). The adopted normative source remains `ember-spec.md`
(`0.8.5_Hardened_1`); executable progress toward H4 is not adoption.

**`ARN-COLL-1` is complete at its defined H4 boundary.** `std.collections`
now supplies the public `Hash` and `Hasher` interfaces and a move-only
`DefaultHasher implements Hasher`. `Hash.hash` is exactly the static generic
`fn hash[H: Hasher](self, mut h: H)` contract. `Hash` is available through the
source-backed prelude; `Hasher`, `DefaultHasher`, and the Arena collection
types remain explicit imports. `finish` consumes the hasher, byte writes do
not retain their input span, equivalent input sequences are deterministic,
and no test or compiler rule freezes the current mixer.

`ArenaMap[K, V]` now accepts both compiler-known keys and user-defined
`K: Eq + Hash`. For a custom key, type checking selects the concrete `Eq.eq`
definition and carries it through HIR into deterministic MIR lowering; no
bytewise or guessed structural comparison exists. The current fixed-capacity
Map is a permitted compact-prefix linear implementation and therefore need
not call the hasher or promise a hash-call count. Safe lookup and iteration
expose keys only through borrowed/shared access. Replacement, removal, result
construction, and suffix compaction preserve move-only keys and values rather
than copying them.

**Source-backed prelude loading is availability, not code reachability.** The
driver loads `std.core` and `std.collections` so their actual interface
definitions back prelude names, but executable C includes only transitively
referenced standard bodies (plus standard drop roots). Analysis and
`--emit mir` continue to see the complete checked program. This keeps an
unused `DefaultHasher` from inserting dormant panic paths into unrelated
generated C.

**Defects found and closed while integrating the surface:**

- D-054: unreachable standard bodies polluted unrelated generated C;
- D-055: ArenaMap internals assumed move-only keys/values were `Copy`;
- D-056: built-in `Eq + Hash` capability failed through a generic bound;
- D-057: every concrete-instantiation diagnostic except E3090 was discarded;
- D-058: an explicitly imported prelude interface kept a second unqualified
  identity and caused false E2070 ambiguity.

All five were compiler defects against already-settled rules. No specification
or ADR changed. The lasting generic lesson is that a fact is not complete when
inference solves it; it is complete only when explicit arguments, substitution,
expected callable types, interface capability, concrete rechecking,
diagnostics, and every downstream IR consumer observe the same fact.

**Permanent adversarial evidence** now covers the entire public Hasher method
surface, custom concrete hasher monomorphization, prelude/non-prelude identity,
move-after-`finish`, rejection of a concrete-Hasher substitute for the generic
Hash signature, missing Eq and Hash bounds, custom move-only Map keys/values,
duplicate replacement, removal/compaction, generic wrappers, read-only
resident keys, and a concrete-only view-storage diagnostic that was formerly
lost. The existing imported-`Ord` test caught D-058 and remains the regression.

**Verified state:** `cargo build --workspace --locked` is warning-free;
`cargo test --workspace --locked` reports 183 tests green, including the full
conformance walk over 95 top-level rule directories and 305 `.em` files. The
test build retains its one pre-existing non-snake-case test-name warning. All
six adopted-source gates pass. Generated C for the custom-key Map passes Clang
with `-std=c11 -pedantic -Wall -Wextra -Werror`. The defect ledger now contains
72 closed defects and none open; deviations D1–D4 remain open; no owner
semantic/API decision is open.

**Boundary of the completion claim:** general automatic/derived `Hash`
generation and ordinary `Map`/`Set` remain later-phase work. This checkpoint
does not claim full H4 adoption, the Phase 4 derive system, or every standard
library type's future Hash implementation.

**Phase count: exactly 1 of 9 phases is complete.** Phase 2 remains active and
substantial; Phases 3–9 have not passed their exit gates.

**Exact next task: resume `ARCH-096-1`.** Select one existing semantic fact,
identify its producer and every consumer, route it through the shared H1
